- **Capacity measurement (C19, this Windows laptop, one API process, FAISS, PostgreSQL 18 review cluster; `scripts/load_test_review_stack.py`, evidence `.verification-live-review-20261029/evidence/load-test-review-stack-single-process.json`):** 50 concurrent sign-ins all succeeded, ~11 logins/s (Argon2), p95 4.3 s. Authenticated read waves (profile, library, conversation list), 3 requests per simulated user: 100 users p50 0.45 s / p95 1.2 s / 0 errors; 250 users p95 3.8 s / 0 errors; 500 users p95 5.2 s / 0 errors; 1,000 simultaneous users p50 4.4 s / p95 12.3 s / 1 error in 3,000. Sustained throughput ~145–177 req/s; per-route ceiling `/health` ~470 req/s, `/api/auth/me` ~160 req/s (session lookup per request). Bursts above ~300 simultaneous sockets hit Windows Proactor `WinError 10054` resets — a host limit, so these are lower bounds, not Linux/server figures. LLM chat was not measured (provider balance).
- **Architectural scaling finding:** a 4-worker uvicorn API against the same data directory refused to start with `IndexLockedError` — `build_rag` takes a per-data-directory `IndexOwnerLock` for FAISS **and** Qdrant, and the Dockerfile pins `--workers 1`. The current design is one API process per installation (workers can be replicated). Supporting 1,000+ simultaneous active users needs multi-process API ownership of indexes/background loops (e.g. Qdrant server as shared index, background outbox/index sync moved to the worker role) and a measured run on target Linux hardware; not changed without Qdrant-server acceptance (Docker Engine unavailable).
- **Multi-process API (owner request: 1,000+ employees):** implemented in-memory **vector index replicas**. New setting `RAG_INDEX_ROLE` (`owner` default = unchanged fail-fast behaviour; `auto` = first process owns the index files, the rest become replicas; `replica`). `FaissVectorStore(persist=False)` never reads or writes the owner's snapshot/change-log files and is filled from PostgreSQL (the source of truth) by the existing `IndexSynchronizer`; a replica fills its index before serving and keeps a sync loop even when `RAG_INDEX_SYNC_SECONDS=0`. Replicas run no background workers (agent queue, embedded ingestion, reminders, automation, outbox) so scheduled jobs cannot run twice. `/health/ready` reports `index_role`. Replica roles are validated to FAISS only (Qdrant multi-process not changed). Partner search already rebuilds lazily from PostgreSQL; the knowledge store is not used for search (PostgreSQL FTS). Files: `app/rag/vector_store.py`, `app/rag/components.py` (`claim_index_role`), `app/main.py`, `app/core/config.py`, `app/api/routes/health.py`.
- Tests: new `tests/unit/test_index_replica.py` (replica writes no files; ignores owner files; aborted rebuild stays empty and retries; `owner` still fails fast while another process holds the lock, `auto`/`replica` become replicas, `auto` becomes owner after release — cross-process via `run_isolated`; qdrant+auto rejected) and `tests/test_ingestion_worker.py::test_replica_api_process_fills_its_index_from_the_database_and_writes_no_index_files` (replica API: health role, upload → separate worker → replica index equals DB chunks, no lock, no index files): **6 passed** with the existing worker-mode test. Regression (ingestion worker, API, Qdrant store, RAG, partner portal/ingestion, knowledge, on-prem config): **103 passed, 1 skipped**; on-prem/deployment-template config **8 passed**.
- Acceptance on this laptop: a 4-worker uvicorn API (`RAG_INDEX_ROLE=auto`, port 63021, pool 4+2 per process) beside the 63011 owner started all four processes as replicas, each rebuilt 2 vectors from PostgreSQL (= DB chunks); readiness `index_role: replica`. Same load test (`load-test-review-stack-four-process-replicas.json`) vs single process: 100 users p95 1.35 s (1.22 s), **250 users 294 req/s and p95 1.85 s (was 145 req/s, 3.85 s)**, 500 users p95 4.0 s (5.2 s), 1,000 users p50 3.1 s / p95 10.1 s / 9 errors in 3,000 (Windows socket resets). Load generator, PostgreSQL and five API processes shared one 6-core/32 GB laptop, so gains are host-capped; target Linux hardware must be measured before quoting a user count. The test API was stopped afterwards; 63011/5182 remain.

- **Usage report provider list fix (2026-10-30):** Cause: `rag_traces.provider/model` were set only from a successful answer (`Trace.record_result`), so failed model calls (all 6 recent calls on 5182 were DeepSeek `LLMUnavailableError`/HTTP 402) had `provider = NULL` and the report's provider query (`provider IS NOT NULL`) returned nothing while the summary counted the calls. Fix in `app/rag/tracing.py`: `TracedLLM.chat` now attributes each call to the client's `provider`/`_model` (string values only) even when the call fails, and `record_result` no longer overwrites a recorded provider with `None`. New test `test_a_failed_model_call_is_attributed_to_its_provider_in_usage_reports` — fails with the fix disabled, passes with it. `tests/test_tracing.py`, conversations, conversation context, voice question API, answer eval and report export tests: **41 passed**. Review API on 63011 restarted (ready 200). Existing traces are not backfilled (their provider is unknown), so the 5182 provider table fills from the next model call; no new call to DeepSeek was made by the agent to demonstrate it.

- **Audit 2026-10-30 P1 — Approvals 502 (root cause, fix, retest):** Reproduced now: Approvals' 7 governance calls all returned 200 in < 200 ms. Root cause from logs: every 502 was Vite's proxy `connect ECONNREFUSED 127.0.0.1:63011`, i.e. the API process was not running (windows 12:37, 1:09–1:15, 2:02–2:15, 4:31); the governance routes were not faulty. The review processes were unsupervised. Fixes: (1) `scripts/supervise_live_review_stack.py` checks API `/health`, the UI and the worker every 10 s and restarts what is down (hung = alive but silent 60 s), recording each restart in `logs/supervisor.log`; verified twice by killing the API — back in ~24 s and ~18 s with a log line. (2) The UI now says "The server could not be reached; it may be restarting. Try again in a few seconds." for a 502/503/504 without an API error body instead of "Request failed (502)". Production Compose already uses `restart: unless-stopped`. Limitation: the supervisor itself does not survive a Windows reboot (observed 18:23 reboot); a scheduled task or service is needed for that.
- **Audit P1 — 87 s retrieval (root cause, fix, retest):** Traces showed warm retrieval at 50–170 ms but 21 s / 36 s / 87 s on the first question after an API start. Root cause: the API loaded the embedding model lazily on the first question; measured cold cost here: torch import 5.6 s + sentence-transformers import 21.8 s + load 0.4 s (44.3 s under load). Fix: `RAG_EMBEDDING_WARMUP` (default on) loads the model in a background task at API startup (failure only logged). Tests: `tests/test_embedding_warmup.py` (3; the startup test fails with the warm-up disabled). Live: log "Embedding model warmed up in 44.3s"; the next two chats' retrieval stages were 336 ms and 301 ms.
- **Audit P1 — AI chat HTTP 402 / local fallback (fix, live test):** New `LLM_FALLBACK_PROVIDER` (`app/services/llm_fallback.py`): when the chosen provider fails with unavailable (incl. 402), timeout or rate limit, the same prompt is answered once by the named provider; invalid input and policy blocks never fall back; a missing or blocked fallback leaves the primary unchanged. Applies to the model picked in chat too (chat always sends a pick), except when the fallback itself was picked. Tests: `tests/unit/test_llm_fallback.py` (8). Live on 5182 with Ollama `qwen2.5:3b` added as custom provider "Local Ollama" (`http://127.0.0.1:11434/v1`, trusted via `PROVIDER_TRUSTED_PRIVATE_ORIGINS`; activated once so its key is active, then DeepSeek re-activated): the audit's synthetic question returned "Record ID QA-ORBIT-4381 … code phrase juniper-4381" with citation `audit-synthetic-20261030.txt · Lines 1–9`, labelled "Local Ollama · qwen2.5:3b"; log shows DeepSeek HTTP 402 then "answering with fallback provider"; trace status ok, provider custom-local-ollama-qwen2-5-3b, retrieval 301 ms, LLM 42.4 s (CPU 3B model, first load). Known gaps: the custom-provider form requires a ≥16-character key even for keyless local endpoints (a placeholder was used); a fallback answer's trace counts one LLM call although two were attempted; the DeepSeek balance itself still needs the owner to top up.

The company brand color now sets `--brand` and each theme derives its primary from it, fixing low-contrast dark-teal accents on dark surfaces and white-on-light primary buttons in dark. Admin Home becomes a live dashboard once setup is complete (`HomeDashboard.tsx`): six KPI tiles (people, departments, documents, questions and answer success over 14 days, AI model), a 14-day answered/failed chart, a prioritised Needs-attention list and recent audit activity — all from real APIs, with "—" when a source cannot be read; the setup checklist collapses to one line with Review setup. Fixes: the blob URL was revoked immediately after `click()`, which some browsers treat as a cancelled download — now revoked after 30 s (Reports and Tender/RFP downloads); a success toast names the file and size. Clicking Export CSV showed "Report downloaded … (1 KB)" and the export request returned 200 in 166 ms. **Not verified:** the file landing on disk — the in-app browser pane does not save downloads to the Windows Downloads folder (only older 16:12 copies exist there).
- **Audit P2 — English/Urdu OCR:** Reproduced with synthetic scans rendered in the browser (correct Urdu shaping) and uploaded through the real pipeline: English text was read exactly; the Urdu scan FAILED (default PP-OCRv6 model reads Latin/Chinese only; the image then fell back to AI description, which was unavailable). Fix: `RAG_OCR_URDU` reads each page with the default and RapidOCR's official Arabic-script recognizer (`arabic_PP-OCRv5_rec_mobile.onnx`, fetched by RapidOCR from its pinned v3.9.2 model registry with SHA256 check; `python-bidi` declared, already hash-pinned in requirements.lock), keeps the reading that recovers more of its script, and maps Arabic letter forms to Urdu (ي→ی, ك→ک, ه→ہ). Tests: `tests/unit/test_urdu_ocr.py` (4) + existing local image OCR test pass. Live re-upload on 5182: Urdu scan now `ready` with "رسید نمبر | کل رقم ایک لاکہ اثہائیس بزار روپے | ادائیگی تیس دن میں کی جائ" — **87.8% character accuracy, 11/16 words exact** (Urdu-Indic digits and some letters such as ھ/ٹ/ہ missed); English unchanged at 100%. Status: English PASS; Urdu **PARTIAL** (Naskh synthetic sample; real Nastaliq scans not measured). The production image must include the Arabic model file in RapidOCR's models folder before enabling the setting.

- **Audit P1 — Manager/Employee authorization and department isolation (live, 2026-10-30):** `scripts/role_qa_setup.py` created two departments (QA Isolation A/B), a Manager of A, an Employee in A and an Employee in B through the real invitation-accept flow (generated passwords stored only in the protected review fixture, never printed), and one department-restricted synthetic document per department. `scripts/role_qa_acceptance.py` result: **49/49 PASS** API checks — each role signs in through the shared login with its own role; sees its own department document and not the other (list hidden; `GET /documents/{id}` and `/file` → 404); seven Administrator endpoints → 403; the Manager manages only A, reads A members, and is denied B members and adding people to B (404); Employees manage no department (403); removing Employee A from A hides the document on the very next request of the same session (404) and re-adding restores it. With `--chat`: other-department facts never appeared in any answer or citation (**6/6 PASS** over two runs); own-department cited answers PASS for B ("4 days", cited) and for A in the first run (code returned), but A was refused by the answer verifier in the second run with the local 3B model — answer quality, not access. `scripts/browser_role_qa.mjs` (headless Chrome, real login form): Admin lands on Home with the Administration section; Manager A and both Employees land on Knowledge; Manager additionally gets My departments; no non-admin sees Administration items, and opening #/people, #/providers, #/settings, #/reports or #/logs as a non-admin returns to Knowledge. - **Owner-reported: "iss video mai kiya ho rha hai" refused (2026-10-30):** Trace: `refused_uncited_document_question` — passages were found, but the local 3B fallback answered without [S#] tags, so the guard (correctly) withheld it. Fixes: (1) one citation-repair call when a document answer comes back uncited (the repaired answer must still pass every support check; explicit INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE is not repaired); (2) "what is happening in this video", Roman Urdu "video mai/mein kiya/kya ho rha/raha hai" and Urdu "ویڈیو میں کیا ہو رہا ہے" are now overview questions (whole-document answers with sentence pruning). The video's evidence is weak: the Whisper-tiny transcript is unusable English for Urdu speech, and frame OCR shows a ZKTeco fingerprint-terminal keypad with "Input Admin PWD" and "PWD Error" at 00:10. **Status: guard PASS; this answer BLOCKED on a capable provider** (DeepSeek balance or Gemini); local CPU models are not reliable here.
- **Review-stack supervision (update):** the API and the supervisor were killed again without logs at 20:46 (only the worker survived), consistent with processes being cleaned up with this agent's tool session; after the Windows reboot at 18:23 nothing was running. The supervisor now also starts the review PostgreSQL cluster when it is down, refuses to run twice (lock file), and survives failed checks; it is now launched outside the tool's job. Registering it as a Windows scheduled task (every 5 minutes, per-user, no admin) was **blocked by the agent's permission policy** and is left to the owner (command in the hand-off).
- **Positions and reporting hierarchy (2026-10-30):** Four concepts kept separate — position (job title), software role, department, reporting line. New `positions` table (title unique case-insensitively among active positions, 11 job families, optional level 1–20, description, archive/restore), `users.position_id` and `users.reports_to_id`, and `org_assignment_history` (title and manager e-mail as they read at the time, start/end). Migration `0071_positions_and_reporting` upgraded, downgraded and re-upgraded cleanly on the review DB. APIs: admin-only `/api/admin/positions` (list/create/patch incl. rename, archive, restore), `/positions/library` and `/library/apply` (80 starter positions from CEO/CFO/CTO to Security Guard, Technician, Driver, Intern, Contractor), `PUT /api/admin/users/{id}/org` (rejects self-reporting, inactive managers/accounts, archived positions for new holders, and any reporting loop), `/users/{id}/org-history`; signed-in `/api/org/chart` (email, position, departments, role, manager only). Every change is audited. Positions grant nothing. Tests: `tests/test_positions_hierarchy.py` 5/5 (custom titles, uniqueness, library idempotence, hierarchy and loop rejection, archive/rename keeping history and holders, audit actions, executive title gives no admin access, chart needs sign-in) plus 30 related user/role/SCIM/template tests pass; frontend 8/8, build pass. UI: People & access gains Positions (stats, add form, filterable table with Edit/Archive/Restore, library chips by job family with Add all) and Org chart (stats, collapsible reporting tree with search, Edit dialog with position, loop-free manager choices and history). Live on 5182: library added 80 positions, custom "Biomedical Equipment Officer" created, CEO → Department Manager → Accountant / Data Entry Operator and CEO → Security Guard built (one via the UI dialog, history row recorded), a loop attempt returned 422 with a clear message; role isolation re-run after assigning positions: 49/49 PASS (access unchanged). 375 px clean. Not yet: positions in CSV bulk import and invitations, delegated approval limits by position.
- **Positions and managers on invitations and CSV import (2026-10-30):** Invitations can carry `position_id` and `reports_to_id` (migration `0072_invitation_position`, upgrade/downgrade/upgrade clean on the review DB); only administrators may set them; both must be active when invited. On acceptance they are applied in the same transaction with an assignment-history row; a position archived or a manager deactivated in the meantime is skipped and recorded in the `onboarding.accepted` audit details instead of failing sign-up. CSV preview accepts optional `position` (matched case-insensitively to active titles) and `reports_to` (an active account's email) columns, returning resolved ids or per-row errors; the template includes them. UI: Position and Reports-to selects on One person and Several people invitations; Position and Reports-to columns in the CSV preview; CSV invites pass them through. Tests: `tests/test_invite_position.py` 4/4 plus invite/CSV/user-management/positions suites (12 + 17) pass; frontend 8/8, build pass. Live on 5182: UI invite of a synthetic account with Site Engineer reporting to Manager A, accepted (password generated in-browser, never returned) → org chart shows Site Engineer under Manager A, role still employee, one history row; CSV preview resolved `technician` → Technician and flagged an unknown position and an inactive manager; inviting the valid row produced an invitation carrying Technician and the manager (then revoked). 375 px clean.
- **Approval limits and financial requests (2026-10-30):** The app had no flow carrying money, so Help & requests gained **Purchase request** and **Expense claim** categories with amount and currency (currency defaults to the company currency; amount required only for these, > 0). New `approval_limits` (per position or per person, per category, amount + ISO currency; one active limit per subject/category; removal keeps an inactive row). Migration `0073_approval_limits` (adds request amount/currency, widens the category check, adds a category/amount consistency check) upgraded, downgraded and re-upgraded cleanly on the review DB. Approving (`resolved`) a financial request now requires approval authority in addition to managing the request: a personal limit overrides the position limit; no limit means no authority — administrators included; nobody may approve their own request; the limit currency must match; rejecting needs no authority. Reviewers see why they cannot approve and the nearest person up the requester's reporting line who can. Approvals write `service_request.approved` audit events with amount, limit and its source. Admin APIs `/api/admin/approval-limits` (list/create/change/remove). UI: amount/currency fields and amount badge in Help & requests, "Approved" status label, approval explanation; People & access → Approval limits tab (form, table, change dialog). Tests: `tests/test_approval_limits.py` 4/4 (within/above limit, rejection, escalation suggestion, admins need a limit, self-approval, currency mismatch, validation, admin-only limits, duplicates, removal) plus service-request, automation (11, 1 skipped), positions and invitation suites pass; frontend 8/8, build pass. Live on 5182: limit set via UI (Department Manager, PKR 50,000); `scripts/approval_limits_qa.py` — Employee A submitted PKR 30,000 and PKR 120,000; Manager A approved 30,000 (200) and was refused 120,000 (403, "This needs PKR 120,000.00; your limit is PKR 50,000.00"); the admin, without a limit, saw "You have no approval limit…"; after a personal PKR 1,000,000 limit was added via UI the admin approved the large request from the triage panel (status resolved; audit limit 1000000.00, source personal). 375 px clean. Not yet: multi-step approval chains (several approvers in sequence) and budget tracking.
- **Multi-step approval chains (2026-10-31):** Administrators configure ordered steps per category (purchase / expense): the requester's manager, any holder of a position, or one named person, each optionally only from an amount. Migration `0074_approval_chains` (`approval_chain_steps`, `request_approval_steps`) upgraded, downgraded and re-upgraded cleanly on the review DB. On submission the applicable steps are copied onto the request (later chain changes do not move it); a manager step with no manager on file falls to the request department's managers. Steps are decided in order through `POST /api/service-requests/{id}/approval-decision`; any rejection (reason required) rejects the request and cancels later steps; nobody approves their own request or two steps of the same request; the final approver also needs an approval limit covering the amount; triage can no longer approve or reject a chained request directly (409). Step approvers can read the request even outside the department. Without a chain the single-step limit approval still applies. Every decision is audited (`service_request.step_approved/_rejected`, final `service_request.approved` with steps, limit and source). Admin APIs `/api/admin/approval-chain` (list, add, remove, move up/down). UI: step timeline with Approve/Reject on Help & requests cards; Approval chain editor (per category, add, reorder, remove) in People & access → Approval limits. Tests: `tests/test_approval_chains.py` 5/5 (thresholds, order, rejection, separation of duties, final authority, out-of-department approver, snapshot, admin-only, reorder) plus limits, service requests, automation and positions suites: 31 passed, 1 skipped; frontend 8/8, build pass. Live on 5182: chain built in the UI (Manager review always; CEO sign-off from 100,000); `scripts/approval_chain_qa.py` — Employee A submitted PKR 30,000 (1 step) and PKR 150,000 (2 steps); the requester's self-approval was refused (403); Manager A approved step 1 of both — the small request closed as approved, the large moved to CEO sign-off; the admin approved it from the timeline (status resolved; audit: two step approvals and the final approval with steps 2, limit 1,000,000, source personal). 375 px clean. Not yet: parallel steps (any-of/all-of), delegation while an approver is away, reminders for pending steps.
- **Budgets & spend (2026-10-31):** Administrators set department budgets per period (purchase requests, expense claims or both; amount and currency; warn or block). Migration `0075_budgets` upgraded, downgraded and re-upgraded cleanly on the review DB. Approved spend = the department's requests in the budget currency closed as approved within the period; committed = requests submitted within the period that are still being approved (rejected requests release it). The check runs at the final approval only (single-step triage and the last chain step): a blocking budget refuses an approval that would exceed it (403 with the budget name and figures); a warning budget allows it and records `over_budget` on the approval audit. Approvers see a budget line (approved, after this approval, would exceed) on financial request cards. APIs `GET/POST /api/org/budgets`, `PATCH /api/org/budgets/{id}` (change amount/enforcement, close); admins manage and see all, managers see their departments' budgets, others 403; changes audited (`budget.created/changed/closed`). UI: new Budgets & spend page (Operations, admins and managers) with KPIs, approved/committed progress bars, create form with month/quarter/year presets, change dialog and close. Tests: `tests/test_budgets.py` 3/3 plus chains, limits and service requests suites: 18 passed; frontend 8/8, typecheck and build pass. Live on 5182: blocking purchase budget PKR 100,000 for QA Isolation A created from the UI (page showed approved PKR 330,000 from earlier QA approvals, Over budget); `scripts/budget_qa.py` — Employee A submitted PKR 20,000; Employee A's budget list 403; Manager A saw the budget and the request line (after approval 350,000, would exceed, block); Manager A's final-step approval was refused 403 "Approving this would exceed the budget …"; the request was then rejected (200). Not yet: budget alerts at thresholds, fiscal-year settings, budget reallocation/transfers, multi-currency conversion.
- **Approval delegation and reminders (2026-10-31):** Anyone can hand their approval-step decisions to a colleague for a date range (administrators can set it up for anyone; at most a year; no overlapping ranges; not to yourself); either side or an administrator can end it at once. During the range the delegate decides the steps the delegator could decide, with the delegator's approval authority (the final step checks the delegator's limit, not the delegate's), and the step records both (`decided_by` and `on_behalf_of`); delegation is not passed on, the requester never approves their own request even as a delegate, and separation of duties counts both people. Delegates can read the requests they can decide. When a step starts waiting, its approvers and their active delegates get an in-app `approval_step_pending` alert; a worker reminds a step that has waited 24 hours, then every 24 hours, at most 3 times (`approval_step_reminder`, brought back to unread, with a timeline note and audit). Migration `0076_approval_delegation` (table `approval_delegations`; step columns `pending_since`, `reminder_count`, `last_reminded_at`, `on_behalf_of_*`; existing pending steps backfilled) upgraded, downgraded and re-upgraded cleanly on the review DB. APIs `GET/POST /api/org/approval-delegations`, `POST /api/org/approval-delegations/{id}/end`; audit `approval_delegation.created/ended`, `service_request.step_reminder`. UI: Approval delegation card on Help & requests (set, list active/upcoming, end); request alerts titled by type; the step timeline shows "by X for Y", waiting since and reminder count. Tests: `tests/test_approval_delegation.py` 3/3 plus budgets, chains, limits, service requests, SLA trigger, positions and invite suites: 31 passed; frontend 8/8, typecheck and build pass. Live on 5182: the delegation Manager A → Employee B (another department) was set from the UI as admin; `scripts/delegation_qa.py` — Employee A submitted two PKR 15,000 purchases; Employee B saw the delegation, got the pending alert, read the request (200, can decide); B's approval reached the final checks and was refused only by the blocking QA budget (403 budget message); B's rejection succeeded and the step shows "Rejected by qa.employee.b … for qa.manager.a …" in the UI; the second request's step was aged 25 h and one reminder pass sent 1 reminder, received by both Manager A and Employee B (reminder_count 1); it was then rejected as cleanup. 375 px: no horizontal scroll. Not yet: delegation for single-step (no chain) approvals, parallel steps, email delivery of reminders, escalation to the next level after the last reminder.
- **Approval escalation and email (2026-10-31):** After the last reminder (3 by default), when another 24 hours pass without a decision, the step is escalated once: the approvers' own managers (`reports_to`; for a position step, its holders' managers) get an in-app `approval_step_escalated` alert, or the administrators when there are none; the requester is never included. Escalation recipients can read the request to follow it up (e.g. an administrator can set a delegation for the absent approver) but cannot decide the step. Timeline note, audit `service_request.step_escalated`, and `escalated_at` on the step. Email: with `APPROVAL_EMAIL_ENABLED=true` (off by default; uses the existing verified-TLS SMTP settings, which are then required) every pending, reminder and escalation alert also queues a content-free email (no subject, amount or requester; only an 8-character request reference) in the new `approval_emails` outbox; the reminder worker sends it with up to 8 attempts and exponential backoff, and cancels emails for requests closed meanwhile or inactive recipients. The SMTP transport was factored into `deliver_message` shared with assignment reminders. Migration `0077_approval_escalation_email` upgraded, downgraded and re-upgraded cleanly on the review DB. UI: escalation alert title and "escalated <time>" on the step timeline. Help & requests now reloads itself every minute while visible and when the tab regains focus (before, a page left open showed stale requests until Refresh). Tests: `tests/test_approval_delegation.py` 5/5 (adds escalation once to the approver's manager, out-of-department follow-up read without decision rights, email queue/send/retry/cancel with a fake transport) plus budgets, chains, limits, service requests, SLA trigger and assignment-reminder email suites: 30 passed; frontend 8/8, typecheck and build pass. Focus reload verified (an extra `/api/service-requests` fetch on focus). **BLOCKED:** real email delivery was not exercised live — no SMTP server is configured on the review stack and no credentials were entered; email delivery is covered only by the fake-transport test.
- **Parallel approval stages (2026-10-31):** A chain step can run together with the previous one: linked steps form a stage whose steps are decided at the same time, either by all of their approvers (all-of) or by any one (any-of; the other steps of the stage become "not needed"). Stages run in order; any rejection rejects the request and cancels the rest; steps of the last stage are final (their approvers need a covering limit), and the budget check runs on the approval that completes the request. One person still never decides two steps of a request, so an all-of stage of two steps for the same position needs two different holders. Pending steps of a stage each get alerts, reminders and escalation. Migration `0078_parallel_approval_stages` (`parallel_with_previous` and `stage_mode` on chain steps; `stage` and `stage_mode` on request steps, existing ones backfilled as one step per stage) upgraded, downgraded and re-upgraded cleanly on the review DB. Admin API: `parallel_with_previous`/`stage_mode` on add, new `PATCH /api/admin/approval-chain/{id}`; moving or removing steps keeps stages consistent (removing a stage's first step keeps the rest as a stage). UI: chain editor groups stages (link/unlink with the previous step, "All must approve / Any one approves" per stage, checkbox on add); request timeline labels stages with their mode; the decision toast now names the step actually decided. Tests: `tests/test_parallel_approvals.py` 3/3 (all-of order-independent and blocking the next stage; any-of completion and rejection; stage editing and separation of duties inside a stage) plus delegation, budgets, chains, limits, service requests and SLA suites: 27 passed; frontend 8/8, typecheck and build pass. Live on 5182 after restarting API and worker: in the chain editor the admin added "Finance review" (admin) with "decide together", moved it next to Manager review and set Stage 1 to "Any one approves"; Employee A's PKR 15,000 purchase got two pending stage-1 steps (any, final); the admin approved from the timeline: request resolved, Manager review "not needed". For this check the QA budget was set to warn and then back to block, and the Finance review step was removed afterwards so the QA chain is back to Manager review → CEO sign-off (≥100,000). Not yet: a minimum count for any-of (e.g. 2 of 3), stage-level min amounts.
- **Auto-refresh everywhere (2026-10-31):** New shared hook `frontend/src/lib/useLiveRefresh.ts`: a page reloads its data every 60 s while the tab is visible, when the tab or window comes back into view (skipped if it reloaded in the last 10 s), and when the person clicks the nav item of the page they are already on (`requestRefresh()`); reloads never overlap and errors stay with the page. Applied to 30 components: Approvals, Automation, Budgets, Claims, Cloud connectors, Department manager, Forecasts, Handoffs, Home setup, Invitations, Knowledge interviews, Documents, Meetings, Memory, My work, Org knowledge, People, Traces, Workspaces, Agent catalog and its review, Training (plans and deadlines), Help & requests (replacing its own timer), Approval delegation, the four org-structure sections (positions, org chart, approval limits, approval chain), role templates, policy impact, folder sync, vector store, and the console's provider and audit data. Pages whose reload shows a loading state (My work, Home setup, Cloud connectors, Forecasts, Automation) reload silently in the background, so no skeleton or disabled buttons flash. Left out on purpose: forms that load saved values into fields being edited (Company setup, Settings, Expertise profile, provider forms) and the on-demand usage report. Frontend 8/8, typecheck and build pass. Live on 5182: on Budgets & spend a budget amount changed outside the page (API) appeared after the window regained focus without pressing Refresh (100,000 → 125,000), and changing it back appeared after clicking the already-active "Budgets & spend" nav item; on My work a refresh request refetched its data with no skeleton shown (sampled every 60 ms).
- **Custom request forms (2026-10-31):** Administrators design forms on a new Request forms page (Operations): name, instructions, the kind of request (category) and optionally the department it goes to, plus up to 30 typed fields — short text, paragraph, number, date, choice (2–50 options) and yes/no ("must be ticked" when required) — with help text, reordering and a live preview. Employees choose a form under Help & requests ("General request" stays the default); the form fixes the category and, when set, routes to its department even if the requester is not a member. The server validates every answer (required, type, option, date, length, unknown keys) and stores the labels and answers as submitted, so editing a form (which starts a new version) never changes earlier requests; archiving hides it from new requests. If "Anything else" is left empty the answers become the request text. Purchase/expense forms still need an amount and follow limits, chains and budgets. Reviewers see the answers as a labelled list on the request card. Migration `0079_request_forms` (table `request_forms`; `form_id`, `form_name`, `form_version`, `form_answers` on `service_requests`) upgraded, downgraded and re-upgraded cleanly on the review DB. APIs: `GET/POST /api/admin/request-forms`, `PUT/DELETE /api/admin/request-forms/{id}`, `GET /api/service-requests/forms`; audit `request_form.created/changed/archived` and the form and version on `service_request.submitted`. Tests: `tests/test_request_forms.py` 3/3 (builder rules and unique names, admin-only, six invalid answer cases, routing to a non-member department, versioning keeps answers, archived form refused, financial form needs an amount, department manager sees answers) plus service requests and all approval suites: 30 passed; frontend 8/8, typecheck and build pass. Live on 5182 after restarting API and worker: "New laptop (QA)" (IT, to QA Isolation B; Model choice, Needed by date, return-device confirmation, all required) was built in the UI; the admin chose it on Help & requests, filled it and submitted — the card shows the three answers and the request went to QA Isolation B; Employee A (not a QA B member) got 422 "Choose one of the options for “Model”" for an invalid option and 201 for a valid one, routed to QA Isolation B. Both test requests were cancelled; the QA form is kept so it is visible in the app. Fixed during the check: form controls now carry their field label for screen readers. 375 px: no horizontal scroll on either page. - **Form attachments (2026-10-31):** New "File upload" field type for request forms. Files are uploaded as they are chosen (`POST /api/service-requests/attachments`) into private storage under random names (`data/request_attachments`, the existing `DocumentStorage`; the client filename is display metadata only, sanitized), then linked to the request when it is submitted — only the uploader's own unsubmitted files can be linked, each once. Accepted: PDF, Word, Excel, PowerPoint, text, CSV, PNG, JPEG up to 10 MB each, with the content checked against the type (PDF/PNG/JPEG/Office signatures; UTF-8 without NUL for text/CSV); up to 5 files per field and 20 unsubmitted uploads per person. An unsubmitted file can be removed (`DELETE …/attachments/{id}`); uploads never submitted are deleted (row and file) after 24 hours by the worker. Download `GET /api/service-requests/{id}/attachments/{file}` only for people who can read the request (others get 404), always as an attachment with `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `Cache-Control: private, no-store`. The answer stores file names, sizes and ids with the request. Migration `0080_request_attachments` upgraded, downgraded and re-upgraded cleanly on the review DB. UI: file picker with per-file remove and limits text on the request form (disabled in the builder preview); request cards list the files as download links. Tests: `tests/test_request_attachments.py` 2/2 (rejects .exe, a fake PDF, a PNG with GIF content and an 11 MB file; strips path from the name; removed and already-used files refused; required file field; download content and headers; unknown file 404; a non-reader cannot download; another user cannot attach someone else's upload; orphan cleanup deletes row and file) plus request forms (updated to ignore the new attachments key) and all request/approval suites: 31 passed; frontend 8/8, typecheck and build pass. Live on 5182 after restarting API and worker: the admin added a "Quotation" file field to "New laptop (QA)" in the builder (version 2; preview upload disabled); on Help & requests chose a PDF in that field (listed as "QA quotation.pdf · 1 KB"), filled the rest and submitted; the card shows the download link and the download returned the same PDF bytes with `attachment` disposition and `nosniff`; Employee A and Employee B (neither can read that request) got 404 for the file. The test request was cancelled. Not scanned for malware — no antivirus engine is configured on this stack (BLOCKED for that check).
- **Conditional form fields (2026-10-31):** A form field can be shown only when an earlier choice or yes/no field has a given answer (`show_if: {field, equals}`); conditions can chain (a field depending on a conditional field is hidden whenever its parent is). The builder offers "Always shown / Only when “X” is…" plus the answer for every field below a choice or yes/no field, and the form list marks forms with conditional fields. The server checks the rule (parent must be above, a choice or yes/no field, and the answer one of its options or Yes/No; references by key, or by label for a field not saved yet) and, on submit, treats hidden fields as not required and drops any answer sent for them — so a hidden file field never links an upload. The request form and the builder preview show and hide fields as answers change, and only shown answers are sent. No migration (the rule lives in the form's field list). Tests: `tests/test_conditional_fields.py` 2/2 (hidden field not required and its answer dropped; required once shown; chained yes/no condition; invalid rules refused) with request forms and attachments: 7 passed; frontend 8/8, typecheck and build pass. Live on 5182 after restarting API and worker: in the builder "Quotation" on "New laptop (QA)" was set to show only when Model is Developer (saved as version 3, rule `model = Developer`); the preview hid it until Developer was chosen; on Help & requests the Quotation picker was absent for Standard and appeared for Developer; a submission with Model Standard that still sent a (fake) quotation id was accepted with only Model, Needed by and the confirmation stored, then cancelled.
- **Form access control (2026-10-31):** A request form can be limited to listed departments and/or positions ("Who can use this form" in the builder: department and position chips; empty means everyone). Members and managers of a listed department, and holders of a listed position, may use it; administrators always may. Forms outside a person's audience are not offered (`GET /api/service-requests/forms` filters per user) and submitting one directly is refused with the same "no longer available" message used for archived forms, so the form's existence is not revealed. The builder validates the departments and active positions, shows the audience on each form in the list, and the audit records the audience size. Changing the audience does not start a new form version (questions are unchanged). Migration `0081_request_form_audience` (`request_forms.audience` JSONB, object check) upgraded, downgraded and re-upgraded cleanly on the review DB. Tests: `tests/test_form_audience.py` 2/2 (department-only and position-only forms offered and accepted only for their audience, everyone-form for all, admins see all, unknown department refused, reopening to everyone, department managers included) with conditional fields, request forms, attachments and service requests: 15 passed; frontend 8/8, typecheck and build pass. Live on 5182 after restarting API and worker: before the change Employee A, Employee B and Manager A were all offered "New laptop (QA)"; the admin limited it to QA Isolation B in the builder (list shows "for QA Isolation B"); afterwards only Employee B (QA B member) was offered it and could submit (201, then cancelled), while Employee A and Manager A were not offered it and got 422 when submitting it by id. The QA form stays limited to QA Isolation B.
- **Webhooks (2026-10-31):** Administrators register receivers on a new Webhooks page (Administration) for events `request.submitted`, `request.status_changed`, `request.approved`, `request.rejected`, `request.cancelled` and `approval.step_pending`. Events are queued in the same transaction as the change (triage, multi-step decisions, submission, cancellation), and HR requests never produce events; payloads carry ids, category, status, subject, priority, department, amount/currency, form name and who decided — never the request description. The worker POSTs JSON signed with HMAC-SHA256 over `<t>.<body>` (`X-InfraSpine-Signature: t=…,v1=…`, plus event and delivery-id headers). The signing secret is generated server-side, stored encrypted (AES-GCM, bound to the webhook row), shown once at creation or rotation. Destinations reuse the custom-provider outbound guard: HTTPS only, no private/internal/link-local/metadata addresses, no credentials in the URL, no redirects, DNS checked at connect time; a private origin works only if the deployment lists it in the new `WEBHOOK_TRUSTED_PRIVATE_ORIGINS`. Failed deliveries retry with exponential backoff (30 s doubling, max 6 h) up to 8 attempts; 20 failures in a row pause the webhook with a reason (resume resets it). Page: create (events checkboxes), secret shown once with copy and verification instructions, send test, pause/resume, new secret, delete (pending deliveries cancelled), and a delivery log (event, status, HTTP code, time taken, attempts, next retry, error) with Redeliver. Migration `0082_webhooks` upgraded, downgraded and re-upgraded cleanly on the review DB. Tests: `tests/test_webhooks.py` 4/4 (rejects http, private, metadata, localhost and credential URLs and unknown events; secret shown once and admin-only; signature verifies; HR request not sent; description not in payload; retry with backoff then success; redeliver; pause after 20 failures and resume; delete; chain step-pending, approved, rejected and status-changed events) plus all request, approval, form and SLA suites: 40 passed; frontend 8/8, build pass. Review stack: `WEBHOOK_TRUSTED_PRIVATE_ORIGINS=http://127.0.0.1:63099` for the new loopback test receiver `scripts/webhook_receiver.py`; the supervisor now re-reads review settings each time it restarts a process (it was restarted once, detached, to load this). Live on 5182: "Local QA receiver" created in the UI with five events (secret shown once, not copied out of the browser); Send test plus an IT request submitted and cancelled produced 3 calls at the receiver (test, submitted, cancelled), an HR request submitted and cancelled produced none, no call contained the request description, and all 3 signatures verified in the browser with the shown secret (WebCrypto HMAC); with the receiver forced to answer 500 a test delivery was retried and sent on attempt 2 (log: sent, 2 tries, HTTP 200). The webhook was then paused because the local receiver is temporary.
- **App-wide dropdown migration:** new `frontend/src/components/ui/select-input.tsx` (`SelectInput`) keeps the native `<select>` contract (value, `onChange(event)` with `event.target.value`, `<option>/<optgroup>` children) but renders the Radix dropdown. A paired-tag codemod replaced **59 single-value selects in 21 components**; the one `multiple` select (Proposal coverage source documents) stays native by design. Known trade-off: converted selects no longer use native `required` form validation (server validation unchanged).

## Earlier source continuation — F18 bounded ephemeral voice questions before API acceptance — 2026-10-27

- Added `Knowledge` chat audio-file upload and browser microphone recording controls plus `POST /api/rag/voice-question`. Microphone recordings stop at 120 seconds and are sent as `audio/webm`; the multipart API only accepts supported audio extensions and caps recordings at the smaller of 8 MB and the configured media file limit. Transcription is capped at 120 seconds and 4,000 characters and uses the configured on-disk `faster-whisper` model (`local_files_only=True`). The upload is stored only in a temporary directory, deleted before RAG runs, and Starlette's upload spool is closed. The answer uses the currently selected chat model or the organization's default provider. The UI labels the result as temporary and explains that only transcription is local: the transcript is sent to the configured answer provider and is not added to saved conversation history. Browser microphone interaction has not yet been tested.
- The endpoint calls the existing RAG response path with the authenticated user and optional focused-document ID, so normal retrieval ACL filtering and citation behavior apply. It sends no history or memory service, creates no RAG trace, disables query-analysis correction caching for this transient request, writes only suffix/byte-count/transcript-length audit metadata, and returns `Cache-Control: no-store`. A declared over-limit duration is rejected before Whisper model initialization; unknown-duration streams are bounded while decoding.
- Focused source command `& '.\.venv-verify-312\Scripts\python.exe' -B -m pytest --noconftest -q tests\test_voice_questions.py tests\test_voice_question_upload_unit.py --tb=short` passed **9 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped in 2.88s**. ## Controlled Docker Desktop restart and 18108 outage — 2026-10-26

### Pre-restart evidence

- Read-only host snapshot: Docker Engine 29.8.2, root `/var/lib/docker`, logging driver `local`, 100 running and 126 stopped containers. The 100 running containers covered 24 `infraspine-combined-restore-*` Compose projects, `infraspine-accept-814b61205ffe` (PostgreSQL and Qdrant), and the unlabeled `advisory-postgres` / `advisory-qdrant` containers. The 24 restore labels were `0219535a4057`, `0b3e62100e80`, `13db157094de`, `186050a76f31`, `243bf32a8e40`, `29391d50c031`, `3148f212d616`, `33309e60f704`, `38e44c3af75f`, `39b27ba49ac3`, `40c3c1ba5bd1`, `4799aba31087`, `4c2025abffef`, `4d1f22992e73`, `4f017027907c`, `74e9f21fe3c7`, `8752354bb543`, `8ba6aeabaa16`, `bc01633f9769`, `d128da650489`, `d1f19427fd9e`, `d557a2cac5ec`, `da9f48081cb2`, and `fd751db66ba3`. The preflight's full 100-row output was truncated by the tool; retain this summary as the exact verified count and labels.
- The active `infraspine-combined-restore-243bf32a8e40` project had `api-1` healthy on `127.0.0.1:18108`, `worker-1` healthy, `postgres-1` healthy, and `qdrant-1` with no container healthcheck on `127.0.0.1:16358->6333`; `/health/ready` returned HTTP 200 with database `ok`. The latest checkpoint records image `infraspine-ai:review-20261025-m1-r1`, image ID `sha256:95e6f2ce8259b05f5fca895043b041cdce2d4ad52ddbf379991dc14dc0f7ca1b`, source revision `32919d7a6979157354ae7377ae0c15080a0e9683`; the container image could not be re-inspected during this pass.
- Before restart, `advisory-postgres` was Up 14 hours with no healthcheck and only an internal 5432 port; `advisory-qdrant` was Up 14 hours with no healthcheck and published host port 6333. The separate `infraspine-accept-814b61205ffe` containers were PostgreSQL (`54380`, healthy) and Qdrant (`54381`, no healthcheck).

### Restart outcome and diagnostic boundary

- Used Docker Desktop Troubleshoot > Restart. The UI moved from Engine stopping to Engine starting but stayed there. The supported `docker desktop restart --timeout 120` timed out while stopping active Docker Desktop/backend/build processes. Its two reported `wsl.exe` processes were verified as Docker's `docker-desktop` `vpnkit-bridge`, not Ubuntu. After confirming the Docker VM had no `dockerd`, `containerd`, or Docker socket, and its data disk was mounted read/write, `docker desktop stop --force --timeout 60` completed. This also stopped Ubuntu WSL; I started Ubuntu once with `/bin/true`, and verified it Running. A later `wsl -l -v` showed Ubuntu Stopped; whether this was idle timeout or Docker Desktop startup was not confirmed. No Ubuntu project process was launched by this task.
- Restarted using `docker desktop start --timeout 120`; it failed with `Docker Desktop is still starting: context deadline exceeded`. Current `docker desktop status` is `starting` (session `c12c0750-eecc-44e8-afdf-bddf1389913a`). The Docker VM is Running but has no Engine process or socket. Docker's recent logs show the restart POST timing out on Engine `/ping` and the previous daemon shutdown completing. No root filesystem I/O error was found in the inspected kernel tail: `/dev/sdd` is an ext4 data disk mounted read/write and reported `recovery complete`. This is evidence against a current ext4 read-only remount, not a physical-drive health certification.
- Post-restart `docker info`, `docker ps`, and `docker system df` each remained blocked and their read-only client calls were cancelled; no containers, volumes, images, or VHDX files were removed or moved. The post-restart `/health/ready` request to 18108 returned connection refused. Host port 6333 has no listener. Two host listeners remain on 5432, but the advisory PostgreSQL container did not publish a host port before restart, so these listeners are not attributed to it. C: free space was `23,117,295,616` bytes and D: `32,687,214,592` bytes (about 21.5/30.4 GiB).
- Docker Desktop CLI version is 4.95.0; the UI offered 4.96.3. Docker's [official release notes](https://docs.docker.com/desktop/release-notes/) for 4.96.0 mention fixes for a stuck engine startup caused by an internal-subnet overlap and an engine becoming unreachable when `volumes-from` refers back to itself. The current Engine is offline, so neither condition has been verified here. No update was installed. ## Latest verified continuation — A07 conflict detection and scoped review — 2026-10-25

- Implemented the remaining conflict-detection and human triage slice of A07/C09 in the existing worktree. New migration `0038_knowledge_conflicts` adds conflict candidates and append-only reviewer decisions. Submission compares the new statement with published, non-private knowledge in overlapping category/scope; a deterministic lexical check looks for overlapping subject terms plus differing numbers, negation or paired opposites. Candidate search uses the existing PostgreSQL GIN full-text index. It never alters publication or RAG eligibility; a human must inspect both source-backed statements.
- Review queue/API filters both statement scopes and all source-document ACLs at read and decision time. An active `knowledge.review` grant must cover both sides, and neither author may review. PostgreSQL guards enforce pair compatibility, active grants/template expiry, event-before-state ordering and immutable decision history; audit stores decision and grant IDs without statement text. Approvals UI compares both statements and their cited passages, supports confirm/dismiss with rationale, keeps history, and loads older items with opaque cursors.
- Added migration `0038_knowledge_conflicts.py`, conflict detector/service/schema/API/model/frontend and `tests/test_knowledge_conflicts.py`. ## Latest continuation pass — bounded custom permission templates

- Added migration `0034_custom_role_templates.py`, Admin API routes, service, schemas and the People-page role-template section. Templates can be assigned only to active Manager/Employee accounts and only for existing `knowledge.review` / `knowledge.publish` actions, scoped to local departments the user currently belongs to. Admin is a protected built-in role. Template definitions are immutable in PostgreSQL; activation is separate from definition history.
- Permission grants are linked to one role-template assignment. Revocation updates all linked grants and assignment history atomically. Grants are revoked on built-in role/dept change, account offboarding, and direct Admin removal from a department. The assignment path locks the user row so concurrent department updates cannot leave a stale scoped grant. The UI only offers the selected user's current departments and omits unsupported `data.steward` (the existing app has no authorization consumer for that permission).

- Native Windows Docling previously emitted an access-violation warning despite a successful parent exit. Ingestion parsing already runs in a killable child process with time/memory bounds and retries without Docling. Linux-container parsing is the supported qualification path; native Windows Docling remains unqualified.

The runbook is `docs/docker-vhdx-recovery-plan.md`. Docker Desktop 4.97.0 remains a candidate only, not a guaranteed fix.

## Current continuation — F07 read-only PostgreSQL analysis — 2026-10-26

- Added an optional operator-configured PostgreSQL reporting source, separate by database name from the application DB and configured with a server-side JSON schema/table/column/type allowlist. API accepts only structured row/count/sum/average/min/max/distinct/group/filter requests; it does not accept SQL text. The source URL is a `SecretStr`, unset by default, and Compose passes it to the API only (the worker receives an empty URL and catalog).
- Each query runs in `SET TRANSACTION READ ONLY`, checks PostgreSQL reports `transaction_read_only=on`, verifies a non-privileged role and rejects write/create privileges in business schemas and the selected table. It applies a 5-second configurable `statement_timeout`, maximum 100 rows plus a one-row truncation check, bound filter values, and 1,000-character cell cap. The UI is Admin-only. Results contain configured source/table/query time provenance; `no-store` headers are set and audit metadata omits filter values and result data. Operator configuration and exact grants are documented in `.env.onprem.example` and `docs/on-premises-deployment.md`.
- `tests/test_database_analysis_unit.py` passed **5/5**. `tests/test_database_analysis_api.py tests/test_database_analysis_postgres.py` passed **3 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped in 23.65s** on current source with a synthetic PostgreSQL 18.6 source DB `infraspine_f07_business_20261026_test`, SELECT-only role `infraspine_f07_reader_20261026`, and separate app test DB `infraspine_f07_api_20261026_test`; test migrations ran to 0048. ## Current continuation — F08 folder source race hardening — 2026-10-26

- Hardened `app/rag/bulk_import.py`: folder traversal now rejects symlinks and Windows junctions. Each file open resolves beneath the configured root, rejects reparse points, opens with `O_NOFOLLOW` where available, and compares file identity before/opened/after. A single pinned file handle is hashed and then copied into private storage; parsing reads the saved snapshot rather than reopening the mutable source path. If the source changes while copying, the digest mismatch fails the import. If parsing or persistence fails after staging, the unreferenced stored file is removed. This closes the path-swap gap between enumeration, hash, parse and save.
- New `tests/test_bulk_import_safety_unit.py`: **3 passed** (14.81s, `--noconftest`) with a workspace-local pytest temp root: regular pinned-handle hashing, deterministic simulated path swap rejection/descriptor close, and streaming enumeration of 1,000 synthetic files. Python compilation passed.

The test DB cluster was stopped and retained; no live app DB or Docker was used.

## A02 current-source provider permission pagination and continuation safety — 2026-10-29

- Hardened Google Drive ACL refresh to use the paginated `files/{id}/permissions` endpoint, request `permissionDetails` (including inherited/direct indicators), follow page tokens, reject repeated tokens, and fail closed beyond 10 pages / 1,000 returned permissions.
- Hardened Microsoft Graph ACL refresh to follow bounded permission pages and fail closed beyond 10 pages / 1,000 principals. Graph delta cursors and provider-returned delta/permission continuation links must now use HTTPS, the exact `graph.microsoft.com` host, and the exact endpoint path before the access token is sent. ## A02 bounded provider permission reconciliation — 2026-10-29

- Added migration `0066_cloud_permission_reconciliation` with a separate private inventory cursor on each connector. Google Drive uses bounded paginated `files.list`; Microsoft Graph uses its own initial `root/delta` enumeration while preserving the ordinary incremental-sync cursor. Each normal connector sync reconciles at most one page of 50 items; cursor state stays server-side and the API response/audit details omit provider tokens.
- Reconciliation updates only documents whose provider item appears in the current inventory page. It removes stale provider grants and quarantines the document before asking the provider for current permissions, then restores only the exact mapped provider allow-list intersected with the local department scope. Unknown, empty, link-shared or unmapped permissions stay quarantined. A per-item provider failure stays quarantined and is retried on a later full scan pass. A missing item in inventory never triggers deletion or revocation. Google folder scans currently include direct children only; recursive subfolders remain open.
- Current-source database-free adapter/service/route tests passed **10/10 in 11.58s**. They cover Google pagination and bounds, Graph terminal cursor secrecy/tombstones, absent-item non-destruction, retry after a transient permission failure, route progress and private-cursor serialization. Evidence: `D:\InfraSpineQA\a02-reconciliation-evidence-20261029-r2\a02-reconciliation-evidence.json`; captured test output and source hashes are in that folder. `0066` upgrade rendered offline through Alembic and includes the expected new column; no database was changed and actual upgrade/downgrade remain unverified. Frontend typecheck passed, existing Node frontend tests passed **8/8**, and production build passed (74 modules; 704.10 kB main chunk with the existing Vite size advisory). Build output is isolated at `D:\InfraSpineQA\a02-reconciliation-unit-20261029\frontend-dist-fresh`.
- A02 remains **partial** for real provider credentials/customer group crosswalk acceptance, database migration execution/rollback, recursive Google folder scope, network-folder support and scheduled end-to-end sync. Existing 5177/5178/5187/63015 services were not restarted or modified. ## C04 SCIM group sync and offboarding continuation — 2026-10-29

- Fixed a concrete deprovision bypass: SCIM `DELETE /Users/{id}` and `PATCH active=false` now use the existing Admin offboarding transaction. It revokes sessions and active role-template assignments and transfers restricted/shared company knowledge to a deterministic active Admin successor. SCIM audit entries identify the `SCIM directory`; the current grant schema requires its `revoked_by` foreign key, so it points to that successor. A shared PostgreSQL transaction advisory lock serializes SCIM and Admin offboarding and protects the last-active-Admin check. Repeated SCIM delete of an already inactive user remains idempotent.
- Added SCIM 2.0 `/Groups` list/create/read/patch/delete routes, Group schema discovery and exact `displayName`/`externalId` filters. Imported groups use directory-owned `custom` kind; memberships use validated local SCIM User IDs, reject unknown users, replace/add/remove atomically, and are limited to 1,000 members per request/resource response. Delete archives the group, removes memberships to revoke group-derived access, and records aggregate counts only. Archived group IDs can be reactivated by a later source sync; local groups cannot be re-owned by SCIM.
- The first focused run found a missing SQLAlchemy `delete` import in the group archive path; that was fixed and the complete focused set then passed **11/11 in 9.99s**. Covered SCIM DELETE/PATCH offboarding delegation and last-Admin/idempotence guards, Groups service membership add/filter/remove/archive, the resolved-user-ID source ACL sync helper, protected FastAPI TestClient route/auth/schema contract, plus the two affected existing SCIM user/API flows. Python compilation and `git diff --check` passed. Ruff is not installed in `.venv-verify-312`. Tests used async DB doubles; no live/test DB, live SCIM credential, browser, or service process was used.
- Source/test hashes and run metadata: `D:\InfraSpineQA\c04-scim-focused-ec0175f2423d41a49aff3b3d3ec973c7\c04-scim-acceptance-evidence.json`, SHA-256 `ef2be1235e3e02263842a8779eb706dbad20832777d123f7ef0f35893250a2ae`.
